## Adversarial Review: AST Extraction Protocol Changes

Reviewing two files:
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`

---

## Critical Issues

### 1. All `$$PARAMS` in YAML rule recipes are the wrong variadic syntax (Confidence: 95)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`

**Lines:** 100, 112, 154, 156, 169, 171, 182, 200, 209 — every YAML recipe block and the inline MCP example

**Problem:** The variadic (zero-or-more nodes) metavariable in ast-grep is `$$$NAME` (three dollar signs). `$$NAME` (two dollar signs) is a distinct feature that captures unnamed tree-sitter nodes, not a sequence of parameters. Every parameter-list placeholder in the new content uses `$$PARAMS`, `$$BASES`, which is the wrong syntax. These patterns will either fail to match correctly or produce unexpected behavior.

The existing pre-change content in the same file (the Forge Tier section, line 45) correctly uses `$$$PARAMS`:
```
- JS/TS: `export function $NAME($$$PARAMS): $RET`
- Rust: `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET`
```

The new content contradicts the existing correct usage in the very same file. This is internally inconsistent and technically broken.

**Affected patterns:**
- MCP example: `"async def $NAME($$PARAMS)"` → should be `$$$PARAMS`
- Inline YAML in `find_code_by_rule` example: `'def $NAME($$PARAMS)'` and `'def _$NAME($$PARAMS)'`
- Python public functions recipe: both `def $NAME($$PARAMS)` and `def _$NAME($$PARAMS)`
- Python public classes recipe: both `class $NAME($$BASES)` and `class _$NAME($$BASES)`
- JS/TS exported functions recipe: `export function $NAME($$PARAMS)`
- Rust recipe: `pub fn $NAME($$PARAMS) -> $RET`
- Go recipe: `func $NAME($$PARAMS) $RET`

**Fix:** Replace every `$$PARAMS`, `$$BASES` with `$$$PARAMS`, `$$$BASES` throughout the new section. The Rust and Go recipes also lack the return type in realistic signatures — Rust functions without `-> $RET` will not match functions that do have return types, and vice versa. Consider using `$$$` (unnamed wildcard) if the return type is optional: `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS)` to match both.

---

### 2. The `not: pattern: 'def _$NAME(...)'` pattern will not work as intended (Confidence: 90)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`

**Lines:** 155-156, 169-171, and the inline YAML at line 112

**Problem:** ast-grep metavariables cannot be mixed with prefix or suffix literal characters as part of the same token. The pattern `def _$NAME(...)` attempts to treat `_$NAME` as a metavariable prefixed with an underscore, but this is explicitly not supported — the documentation states "Meta variables cannot be mixed with prefix/suffix strings." The `_` before `$NAME` is part of the same identifier token in the AST, so ast-grep will parse `_$NAME` as a literal string followed by a metavariable token boundary, which is not a valid construct.

The correct way to exclude private functions in Python is via the `constraints` field on the metavariable, using a regex that requires the captured name does NOT start with an underscore:

```yaml
rule:
  pattern: 'def $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
  inside:
    kind: module
    stopBy: end
constraints:
  NAME:
    regex: '^[^_]'
```

Or use the `not` composite rule combined with the `regex` atomic rule on the node text. The current approach with `not: pattern: 'def _$NAME(...)'` is the approach one might expect to work intuitively, but it relies on an invalid pattern (`_$NAME` as a mixed literal+metavariable token). An LLM following this recipe will likely see zero matches from the `not` branch (because the pattern is invalid and never matches), causing private functions to pass through — a silent functional failure, not a parse error.

This same bug appears in both the Python functions recipe, the Python classes recipe, and the inline `find_code_by_rule` YAML example. All three need the same fix.

---

### 3. The Rust YAML recipe misses functions without return types (Confidence: 85)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`

**Lines:** 198-201

**Problem:**
```yaml
rule:
  pattern: 'pub fn $NAME($$PARAMS) -> $RET'
```

This pattern requires `-> $RET` to be present. Rust functions returning the unit type `()` are written as `pub fn name(params)` with no `->` arrow at all. This pattern will silently skip all `pub fn` functions that return nothing (unit), which is a very common case (constructors, event handlers, side-effectful functions). In a real codebase this can miss a significant fraction of the public API.

**Fix:** Use two patterns combined with `any`:
```yaml
rule:
  any:
    - pattern: 'pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET'
    - pattern: 'pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
```

Or accept that `$RET` must also be fixed once `$$PARAMS` → `$$$PARAMS` is applied. Either way, this pattern does not cover the unit-return case.

---

### 4. Duplicate section numbering in step-03-extract.md (Confidence: 88)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`

**Lines:** 79 and 149

**Problem:** The MANDATORY SEQUENCE contains two sections both numbered "4":
- `### 4. Execute Tier-Dependent Extraction` (line 79)
- `### 4. Build Extraction Inventory` (line 149)

The original file had the extraction as step 4 and the inventory build as step 5. The new content added "Check for Docs-Only Mode" as step 3 (line 69), causing the former step 4 ("Execute Tier-Dependent Extraction") to remain as 4, but the subsequent "Build Extraction Inventory" was not renumbered — it should now be step 5. The Gate 2 summary and menu are referenced as "Section 5" in the cleanup note (line 113): "before presenting the Gate 2 summary (Section 5)". That reference is now wrong; the Gate 2 summary is at what is effectively section 6 if renumbering is done correctly.

An LLM interpreting this sequence could get confused by the duplicate "4" and the mismatch with the "Section 5" cross-reference in the cleanup note.

**Fix:** Renumber the sequence: step 3 = Docs-Only Check, step 4 = Execute Tier-Dependent Extraction, step 5 = Build Extraction Inventory, step 6 = Present Summary (Gate 2), step 7 = Present Menu Options. Update the "Section 5" reference in the cleanup note to "Section 6".

---

## Important Issues

### 5. Decision tree threshold ambiguity: ≤100 and ≤500 overlap at the boundary (Confidence: 82)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`

**Lines:** 73-87

**Problem:** The decision tree reads:
```
Files in scope ≤ 100  → find_code()
Files in scope ≤ 500  → find_code_by_rule()
Files in scope > 500  → CLI streaming
```

A repo with exactly 100 files matches both the first AND second conditions. The intent is clearly mutually exclusive tiers (using else-if semantics), but the prose format does not make the else-if structure explicit. An LLM following this literally could pick the wrong branch for 100 files (the second branch also technically applies). The step-03 summary (lines 130-132) does correctly use `≤100` / `≤500` / `>500` sequentially, which implies else-if, but the data file itself is ambiguous.

The step-03 wording in the summary bullet matches the protocol, so the primary risk is in the data file being read as standalone. A minor fix would be to write the second branch as `101–500 files` or add an explicit note: "Apply the first matching condition."

---

### 6. Safety valve threshold (200 entries) is inconsistent with `max_results` caps (Confidence: 80)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`

**Lines:** 91, 75, 79, 136

**Problem:** The safety valve triggers at "200 entries." The `find_code()` MCP call caps at `max_results=100`, and `find_code_by_rule()` caps at `max_results=150`. If `max_results` is already enforced by the MCP tool, the safety valve at 200 can never be triggered by MCP calls — it would only fire for CLI streaming, where `| head -200` is the hard cap. This means the safety valve is dead code for MCP paths. The intent was probably to guard against MCP tools that do not honor `max_results`, but that is not stated. The streaming `| head -200` cap also means the safety valve condition ("exceeds 200 entries") is impossible to reach in the CLI path since `head -200` prevents that.

This is not a breakage — the safety valve simply never fires — but it creates a false sense of protection. If a future author removes the `max_results` cap thinking the safety valve will catch it, extraction will overflow. A note clarifying the relationship between `max_results` and the safety valve would prevent this.

---

### 7. The update-skill workflow's `step-03-re-extract.md` is not updated to reference the AST Extraction Protocol (Confidence: 80)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`

**Lines:** 114-124

**Problem:** The create-skill `step-03-extract.md` now has a hard CRITICAL warning to follow the AST Extraction Protocol before running any ast-grep operation. The update-skill `step-03-re-extract.md` still uses the old generic approach ("Use ast_bridge for full structural extraction") with no reference to the Protocol, no `--json=stream` requirement, and no streaming safeguard. The same root cause that triggered this fix (large-repo overflow from `ast-grep --json`) applies equally to re-extraction during updates. A cognee-sized repo hitting the update workflow would encounter the same failure mode.

The protocol is defined in a create-skill data file (`extraction-patterns.md`) that the update-skill workflow does not reference. There is no `extractionPatternsData` frontmatter in the update-skill step, and no load instruction for that file. The fix is either to (a) move the protocol to a shared data location and reference it from both workflows, or (b) duplicate the critical constraint in `step-03-re-extract.md`'s Forge tier section.

---

| 3 | Rust recipe silently misses `pub fn` with no return type | 85 | Critical |
| 4 | Duplicate section "4" in step-03 MANDATORY SEQUENCE, stale "Section 5" cross-ref | 88 | Important |

| 7 | update-skill `step-03-re-extract.md` not updated; same overflow failure mode unaddressed | 80 | Important |

Issues 1, 2, and 3 represent technically broken YAML recipes that will either silently mismatch or produce wrong results in real usage. Issue 4 creates a structural integrity problem in the step file that could mislead an LLM agent following the sequence. Issues 5-7 are design gaps that do not break the happy path but create known edge-case failure modes.

     8→# Step 3: Re-Extract Changed Exports
     9→
    10→## STEP GOAL:
    11→
    12→Perform tier-aware extraction on only the changed files identified in step 02, producing fresh export data with confidence tier labels (T1/T1-low/T2) that will be merged into the existing skill in step 04.
    13→
    14→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):

    30→- ✅ You bring AST-backed structural analysis; the source code provides ground truth
    31→

    33→
    34→- 🎯 Focus ONLY on extracting changed exports — do not merge or modify existing skill
    35→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to extract unchanged files — only files in the change manifest
    36→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to modify SKILL.md — extraction produces intermediate data for step 04
    37→- 💬 DO NOT BE LAZY — For EACH changed file, launch a subprocess for deep AST analysis (Pattern 2)

    44→- 📖 Label every extraction with confidence tier
    45→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to hallucinate exports — only extract what exists in source
    46→
    47→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    48→
    49→- Available: change manifest from step 02, forge tier from step 01, source files
    50→- Focus: extraction only — no merge, no write
    51→- Limits: only changed files from manifest
    52→- Dependencies: step 02 must have produced change manifest
    53→
    54→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    55→
    56→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
    57→
    58→### 1. Check for Docs-Only Mode
    59→
    60→**If `source_type: "docs-only"` in the original brief or metadata:**
    61→
    62→"**Docs-only skill detected.** This skill was generated from external documentation, not source code. Re-extraction will re-fetch the original `doc_urls` to check for updated content."
    63→
    64→- Re-fetch each URL from `doc_urls` (from the brief or metadata) using whatever web fetching capability is available
    65→- Extract updated API information with T3 `[EXT:{url}]` citations
    66→- Build the updated extraction inventory from fetched content
    67→- Skip all source code extraction below — proceed directly to the merge step (section 5 or equivalent)
    68→
    69→**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue with source extraction below.
    70→
    71→### 1b. Determine Extraction Strategy by Tier
    72→
    73→**Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep only):**
    74→
    75→If `source_root` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
    76→
    77→If `source_root` (from metadata.json) is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:
    78→
    79→1. **Check `git` availability:** Verify `git` is functional (`git --version`). If `git` is not available, skip to the fallback warning below.
    80→
    81→2. **Ephemeral sparse clone:** Clone only the changed files from the change manifest to a system temp path. Note: at this point in the flow, `{source_root}` is known to be a remote URL (the local-path case was already handled above).
    82→
    83→   ```
    84→   temp_path = {system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/
    85→   git clone --depth 1 --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_root} {temp_path}
    86→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {changed_files_from_manifest}
    87→   ```
    88→
    89→   No `--branch` flag is used — the clone targets the remote's default branch, which must match the branch used during the original [CS] Create Skill run. This scopes the clone to only the files identified in step-02's change manifest, avoiding a full repository download.
    90→
    91→3. **If clone succeeds:** Update the working source path to `{temp_path}` for all subsequent AST operations in this step. Proceed with the **Forge tier** extraction strategy below. Mark `ephemeral_clone_active = true` for cleanup.
    92→
    93→4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**
    94→
    95→   ⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:**
    96→
    97→   "Ephemeral clone of `{source_root}` failed: {error}. Degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and re-run [CS] Create Skill with the local path, then re-run this update."
    98→
    99→   Override the extraction strategy to Quick tier for this run. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
   100→
   101→**Ephemeral clone cleanup:** After extraction is complete for all files in scope (whether successful or partially failed), before presenting the extraction summary (Section 5), if `ephemeral_clone_active`, delete the `{temp_path}` directory. Log: "Ephemeral source clone cleaned up." This ensures cleanup runs even if some extractions failed, as long as the step itself is still executing.
   102→
   103→**If AST tool is unavailable at Forge/Deep tier (local source):**
   104→
   105→⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:** "AST tools are unavailable — extraction will use source reading (T1-low). Run [SF] Setup Forge to detect and configure AST tools for T1 confidence."
   106→
   107→Degrade to Quick tier extraction. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
   108→
   109→**Quick tier (text pattern matching):**
   110→- Extract function/class/type names via regex patterns
   111→- Extract export statements via text matching
   112→- Confidence: T1-low (pattern-matched, not AST-verified)
   113→
   114→**Forge tier (AST structural extraction):**
   115→- Use ast_bridge for full structural extraction
   116→- Extract: function signatures, type definitions, class members, exported constants
   117→- Extract: parameter types, return types, JSDoc/docstring comments
   118→- Confidence: T1 (AST-verified structural truth)
   119→
   120→**Deep tier (AST + QMD semantic enrichment):**
   121→- Perform all Forge tier extractions (T1)
   122→- Additionally: launch a subprocess that queries qmd_bridge for temporal context on changed exports, returning T2 evidence per export
   123→- QMD provides: usage patterns, historical context, related documentation
   124→- Confidence: T1 for structural, T2 for semantic enrichment
   125→
   126→### 2. Extract Changed Files
   127→
   128→DO NOT BE LAZY — For EACH file in the change manifest with status MODIFIED, ADDED, or RENAMED, launch a subprocess that:
   129→
   130→1. Loads the source file
   131→2. Performs tier-appropriate extraction (Quick/Forge/Deep)
   132→3. For each export found:
   133→   - Record: export name, type (function/class/type/constant), signature
   134→   - Record: file path, start line, end line
   135→   - Record: parameters with types (if function/method)
   136→   - Record: return type (if function/method)
   137→   - Record: JSDoc/docstring summary (if present)
   138→   - Label: confidence tier (T1/T1-low/T2)
   139→4. Returns structured extraction findings to parent
   140→
   141→**For DELETED files:** No extraction needed — deletions handled in merge step.
   142→
   143→**For MOVED files:** Re-extract at new location to update file:line references.
   144→
   145→### 3. Deep Tier QMD Enrichment (Conditional)
   146→
   147→**ONLY if forge_tier == Deep:**
   148→
   149→Read the `qmd_collections` registry from `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml`.
   150→
   151→Find the collection entry matching the current skill: look for an entry where `skill_name` matches the skill being updated AND `type` is `"extraction"`.
   152→
   153→**If a matching extraction collection is found:**
   154→Launch a subprocess that loads qmd_bridge and for each changed export:
   155→1. Queries the `{skill_name}-extraction` collection for semantic context related to the export
   156→2. Searches for usage patterns, documentation references, temporal history
   157→3. Returns T2 evidence per export (usage frequency, context snippets, related concepts)
   158→
   159→**If no matching collection found in registry:**
   160→Log: "No QMD extraction collection found for {skill_name}. T2 enrichment skipped. Re-run [CS] Create Skill to generate the collection."
   161→Continue without T2 enrichment — extraction still produces T1 results.
   162→
   163→**If forge_tier != Deep:** Skip this section with notice: "QMD enrichment skipped (tier: {forge_tier})"
   164→
   165→### 4. Compile Extraction Results
   166→
   167→Aggregate all subprocess results into structured extraction data:
   168→
   169→```
   170→Extraction Results:
   171→  files_extracted: [count]
   172→  exports_extracted: [count]
   173→  confidence_breakdown:
   174→    T1: [count]
   175→    T1-low: [count]
   176→    T2: [count]
   177→
   178→  Per-file extractions:
   179→    {file_path}:
   180→      exports:
   181→        - name: {export_name}
   182→          type: function|class|type|constant
   183→          signature: {full signature}
   184→          location: {file}:{start_line}-{end_line}
   185→          confidence: T1|T1-low|T2
   186→          parameters: [{name, type}]
   187→          return_type: {type}
   188→          docstring: {summary}
   189→          qmd_evidence: {if Deep tier}
   190→```
   191→
   192→### 5. Display Extraction Summary and Auto-Proceed
   193→
   194→"**Re-Extraction Complete:**
   195→
   196→| Metric | Count |
   197→|--------|-------|
   198→| Files extracted | {count} |
   199→| Exports extracted | {count} |
   200→| T1 (AST-verified) | {count} |
   201→| T1-low (pattern-matched) | {count} |
   202→| T2 (QMD-enriched) | {count} |
   203→
   204→**Proceeding to merge with existing skill...**"
   205→
   206→### 6. Present MENU OPTIONS
   207→
   208→Display: "**Proceeding to merge...**"
   209→
   210→#### Menu Handling Logic:
   211→
   212→- After extraction results are compiled, immediately load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
   213→
   214→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   215→
   216→- This is an auto-proceed step with no user choices
   217→- Proceed directly to next step after extraction completes
   218→
   219→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   220→
   221→ONLY WHEN all changed files have been extracted and results compiled will you load {nextStepFile} to begin the merge operation.
   222→
   223→---
   224→
   225→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   226→
   227→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   228→
   229→- Every changed file from manifest extracted with tier-appropriate method
   230→- All exports labeled with confidence tier (T1/T1-low/T2)
   231→- AST file:line citations on every extracted export
   232→- QMD enrichment performed for Deep tier (skipped with notice for Quick/Forge)
   233→- Extraction results compiled with per-file detail
   234→- Summary displayed before auto-proceeding
   235→- No unchanged files extracted
   236→
   237→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   238→
   239→- Extracting unchanged files (wasting resources)
   240→- Missing confidence tier labels on any export
   241→- Hallucinating exports not present in source code
   242→- Skipping QMD enrichment at Deep tier
   243→- Modifying SKILL.md or any existing artifacts during extraction
   244→
   245→**Master Rule:** Skipping steps, optimizing sequences, or not following exact instructions is FORBIDDEN and constitutes SYSTEM FAILURE.
   246→

+def parse_matches(stdout: str, max_results: int = 0) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
+    """Parse JSONL (--json=stream) output with optional early exit.
+
+    Returns (matches, total_count). Only parses JSON for kept matches;
+    remaining lines are counted but not deserialized.
+    """
+    matches = []
+    total_lines = 0
+    for line in stdout.splitlines():
+        line = line.strip()
+        if not line:
+            continue
+        total_lines += 1
+        if not max_results or len(matches) < max_results:
+            matches.append(json.loads(line))
+    return matches, total_lines
+

> AGENT

Now I have the full picture. Here's the impact analysis:

## Impact on SKF Testing

### What's running locally

Your MCP server config (`ast-grep` in `.claude.json`) points to `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/ast-grep-mcp` and runs via `uv run main.py`. Commit `08c489b` is the streaming fix — so **your MCP tools are already patched**.

### What happens when you test the new SKF extraction protocol

**The two fixes stack — they're complementary, not redundant:**

| Layer | Before | After |
|-------|--------|-------|
| **MCP server** (your fix) | `--json` + `json.loads()` full array | `--json=stream` + `parse_matches()` with early exit |
| **SKF workflow** (our fix) | No protocol — agent improvises | Decision tree + `max_results` + safety valve |

### Concrete impact per decision tree branch

**Branch 1 (≤100 files) — `find_code()` MCP tool:**
- The MCP server now uses `--json=stream` internally + early exit at `max_results`
- SKF passes `max_results=100` → the server stops parsing JSON after 100 matches
- **Double win**: server memory is bounded AND agent context is bounded

**Branch 2 (101-500 files) — `find_code_by_rule()` MCP tool:**
- Same streaming fix applies to `scan` mode too (your PR patches both `run` and `scan`)
- Scoped YAML rules reduce matches at the AST level, streaming handles the rest
- `max_results=150` → server early-exits at 150

**Branch 3 (>500 files) — CLI streaming fallback:**
- This path bypasses the MCP server entirely (direct `ast-grep` CLI)
- SKF's `--json=stream` + Python recipe handles it independently
- Your MCP fix has no effect here — but it doesn't need to

### The key insight

Your MCP fix makes the safety valve in our SKF protocol **nearly unnecessary** for the MCP paths. Before your fix, even with `max_results=100`, the MCP server would still OOM on large repos because it loaded everything first. Now with `parse_matches()`, the server itself does early exit — so the "MCP tool times out on large repo" failure mode that our safety valve guards against is **effectively eliminated**.

The safety valve remains useful only for:
- Edge cases where a small file count produces unexpectedly many matches per file
- Future MCP server versions that might regress
- The CLI fallback path (which doesn't go through MCP)

### One thing to watch during testing

Your `parse_matches()` counts `total_lines` but doesn't skip non-JSON lines. The `--json=stream` output should be clean, but if `ast-grep` ever emits a warning to stdout, `json.loads(line)` would throw. The current code only wraps the kept matches in `json.loads()` and skips for counted-but-not-kept lines — so a warning line after `max_results` would inflate `total_lines` but not crash. Worth a quick test with a repo that triggers ast-grep warnings.

> AGENT

Already handled. `ec365c3` adds `not line.startswith("{")` — non-JSON lines (warnings, empty lines, anything not a JSON object) are skipped before counting or parsing. Clean fix, no edge case left.

> DEVELOPER

everything will work locally as expected without OOM?

> AGENT

Yes. The full chain is now protected at every layer:

1. **MCP server** (`parse_matches`) — `--json=stream` + line-by-line parsing + early exit at `max_results` + non-JSON line skipping → O(min(n, max_results)) memory
2. **SKF workflow** (extraction protocol) — decision tree caps `max_results` at 100/150, safety valve catches edge cases
3. **CLI fallback** (>500 files) — `--json=stream` + streaming Python + `| head -200` → O(1) memory

No path loads the full result set into memory anymore. You're good.

What are your takes on this documentation update?

> AGENT

No documentation updates needed. Here's why:

**The changes are invisible to end users.** The AST Extraction Protocol is internal workflow plumbing — Ferris reads it during `@Ferris CS` / `@Ferris US` execution. Users never see `--json=stream`, decision trees, or YAML rule recipes. They type `@Ferris CS` and it works. The only observable difference: it now works on large codebases that previously would have choked.

**File-by-file assessment:**

| File | Update? | Reason |
|------|---------|--------|
| `README.md` | No | Mentions ast-grep as a tool, not how it's invoked internally |
| `docs/` | No | Architecture-level descriptions, not CLI flag details |
| `medium-article-skf.md` | No | "ast-grep parses the AST" — correct before and after |
| `medium-article-deep-tier.md` | No | Discusses QMD collections and knowledge architecture, not extraction plumbing |

**On the PR situation:** I'd advise against documenting the fork branch. Three reasons:

1. **SKF already has the fallback.** Our CLI streaming protocol (`--json=stream` + Python) kicks in regardless of whether the MCP server is patched. Users with the unpatched MCP server hit the safety valve → CLI fallback → works anyway.
2. **Temporary documentation rots.** "Clone this branch until the PR merges" becomes stale the moment the PR is accepted or rejected.
3. **It's a third-party tool detail.** Telling SKF users to patch their MCP server installation conflates two unrelated concerns.

If the PR merges → users get it on next `uv` update, transparently. If it doesn't → SKF's workflow handles it via the fallback path. Either way, end users are covered.

     1→/**
     2→ * SKF Installer UI - Banner, prompts, and success message.
     3→ * Uses @clack/prompts for terminal UI.
     4→ */
     5→
     6→const { intro, outro, text, select, multiselect, confirm, note, isCancel, cancel, log } = require('@clack/prompts');
     7→const chalk = require('chalk');
     8→const figlet = require('figlet');
     9→const path = require('node:path');
    10→const fs = require('fs-extra');
    11→const yaml = require('js-yaml');
    12→
    13→const SKF_FOLDER = '_bmad/skf';
    14→
    15→class UI {
    16→  displayBanner() {
    17→    let banner;
    18→    try {
    19→      banner = figlet.textSync('SKF', { font: 'Standard' });
    20→    } catch {
    21→      banner = '\n  S K F';
    22→    }
    23→    const packageJson = require('../../../package.json');
    24→    intro(
    25→      `${chalk.cyan(banner)}\n${chalk.white.bold('  Skill Forge')} ${chalk.dim(`v${packageJson.version}`)}\n${chalk.dim('  AST-verified, provenance-backed agent skills from code\n  repositories, documentation, and developer discourse')}`,
    26→    );
    27→  }
    28→
    29→  async detectInstallation(projectDir) {
    30→    const hasBmadSkf = await fs.pathExists(path.join(projectDir, SKF_FOLDER));
    31→    const hasBmadDir = await fs.pathExists(path.join(projectDir, '_bmad'));
    32→
    33→    if (hasBmadSkf) {
    34→      return { type: 'existing', folder: SKF_FOLDER };
    35→    }
    36→    if (hasBmadDir) {
    37→      return { type: 'bmad-ready', folder: SKF_FOLDER };
    38→    }
    39→    return { type: 'fresh', folder: SKF_FOLDER };
    40→  }
    41→
    42→  async promptInstall() {
    43→    this.displayBanner();
    44→
    45→    const projectDir = process.cwd();
    46→    const defaultProjectName = path.basename(projectDir);
    47→    const detection = await this.detectInstallation(projectDir);
    48→    const skfFolder = detection.folder;
    49→
    50→    log.info(`Target: ${chalk.cyan(projectDir)}`);
    51→
    52→    let action = 'fresh';
    53→
    54→    if (detection.type === 'existing') {
    55→      log.warn(`Found existing installation at ${chalk.white(SKF_FOLDER + '/')}`);
    56→
    57→      const choice = await select({
    58→        message: 'What would you like to do?',
    59→        options: [
    60→          { label: 'Update — Replace SKF files, keep config.yaml', value: 'update' },
    61→          { label: 'Fresh install — Remove everything and start over', value: 'fresh' },
    62→          { label: 'Cancel', value: 'cancel' },
    63→        ],
    64→      });
    65→
    66→      if (isCancel(choice) || choice === 'cancel') {
    67→        cancel('Installation cancelled.');
    68→        return { cancelled: true };
    69→      }
    70→      action = choice;
    71→    } else {
    72→      log.info(`Agents and workflows will be installed in ${chalk.white(skfFolder + '/')}`);
    73→    }
    74→
    75→    if (action === 'update') {
    76→      log.info('Existing config.yaml will be preserved.');
    77→      return {
    78→        projectDir,
    79→        skfFolder,
    80→        _detection: detection,
    81→        _action: action,
    82→        cancelled: false,
    83→      };
    84→    }
    85→
    86→    // Load saved config to pre-populate defaults on fresh reinstall
    87→    const savedConfig = await this.loadSavedConfig(projectDir, skfFolder);
    88→    if (savedConfig) {
    89→      log.info('Previous configuration detected — defaults pre-populated.');
    90→    }
    91→
    92→    const ideOptions = [
    93→      { label: 'Claude Code', value: 'claude-code' },
    94→      { label: 'Cline', value: 'cline' },
    95→      { label: 'Codex', value: 'codex' },
    96→      { label: 'Cursor', value: 'cursor' },
    97→      { label: 'GitHub Copilot', value: 'github-copilot' },
    98→      { label: 'Roo Code', value: 'roo' },
    99→      { label: 'Windsurf', value: 'windsurf' },
   100→      { label: 'Other', value: 'other' },
   101→    ];
   102→
   103→    // Pre-check IDEs: saved config takes priority, then auto-detect from directories
   104→    const savedIdes = savedConfig?.ides || [];
   105→    let initialIdes = [];
   106→    if (savedIdes.length > 0) {
   107→      initialIdes = savedIdes;
   108→    } else {
   109→      const detectedIdes = await this.detectIdes(projectDir);
   110→      if (detectedIdes.length > 0) {
   111→        initialIdes = detectedIdes;
   112→        log.info(`Auto-detected IDEs: ${detectedIdes.join(', ')}`);
   113→      }
   114→    }
   115→
   116→    // Mark initially selected IDEs
   117→    for (const opt of ideOptions) {
   118→      opt.initialSelected = initialIdes.includes(opt.value);
   119→    }
   120→
   121→    // Project name
   122→    const project_name = await text({
   123→      message: 'Project name:',
   124→      initialValue: savedConfig?.project_name || defaultProjectName,
   125→    });
   126→    if (isCancel(project_name)) {
   127→      cancel('Installation cancelled.');
   128→      return { cancelled: true };
   129→    }
   130→
   131→    // Skills output folder
   132→    const skills_output_folder = await text({
   133→      message: 'Where should generated skills be saved?',
   134→      initialValue: savedConfig?.skills_output_folder || 'skills',
   135→    });
   136→    if (isCancel(skills_output_folder)) {
   137→      cancel('Installation cancelled.');
   138→      return { cancelled: true };
   139→    }
   140→
   141→    // Forge data folder
   142→    const forge_data_folder = await text({
   143→      message: 'Where should forge workspace artifacts be stored?',
   144→      initialValue: savedConfig?.forge_data_folder || 'forge-data',
   145→    });
   146→    if (isCancel(forge_data_folder)) {
   147→      cancel('Installation cancelled.');
   148→      return { cancelled: true };
   149→    }
   150→
   151→    // IDE selection
   152→    const ides = await multiselect({
   153→      message: 'Which tools/IDEs are you using?',
   154→      options: ideOptions,
   155→      initialValues: initialIdes,
   156→      required: true,
   157→    });
   158→    if (isCancel(ides)) {
   159→      cancel('Installation cancelled.');
   160→      return { cancelled: true };
   161→    }
   162→
   163→    // Learning material
   164→    const install_learning = await confirm({
   165→      message: 'Install learning & reference material?',
   166→      initialValue: true,
   167→    });
   168→    if (isCancel(install_learning)) {
   169→      cancel('Installation cancelled.');
   170→      return { cancelled: true };
   171→    }
   172→
   173→    return {
   174→      projectDir,

   179→      install_learning,

   181→      _detection: detection,

   184→    };
   185→  }
   186→
   187→  async detectIdes(projectDir) {
   188→    const markers = {
   189→      'claude-code': ['.claude'],
   190→      cursor: ['.cursor'],
   191→      cline: ['.clinerules'],
   192→      codex: ['.codex'],
   193→      'github-copilot': ['.github/copilot-instructions.md', '.github/prompts'],
   194→      roo: ['.roo', '.roomodes'],
   195→      windsurf: ['.windsurf'],
   196→    };
   197→
   198→    const detected = [];
   199→    for (const [ide, paths] of Object.entries(markers)) {
   200→      for (const p of paths) {
   201→        if (await fs.pathExists(path.join(projectDir, p))) {
   202→          detected.push(ide);
   203→          break;
   204→        }
   205→      }
   206→    }
   207→    return detected;
   208→  }
   209→
   210→  async loadSavedConfig(projectDir, skfFolder) {
   211→    const configPath = path.join(projectDir, skfFolder, 'config.yaml');
   212→    try {
   213→      if (await fs.pathExists(configPath)) {
   214→        const content = await fs.readFile(configPath, 'utf8');
   215→        return yaml.load(content) || null;
   216→      }
   217→    } catch {
   218→      // ignore parse errors
   219→    }
   220→    return null;
   221→  }
   222→
   223→  displaySuccess(skfFolder, ides = [], action = 'fresh') {
   224→    const ideNames = {
   225→      'claude-code': 'Claude Code',
   226→      cline: 'Cline',
   227→      codex: 'Codex',
   228→      cursor: 'Cursor',
   229→      'github-copilot': 'GitHub Copilot',
   230→      roo: 'Roo Code',
   231→      windsurf: 'Windsurf',
   232→      other: 'your IDE',
   233→    };
   234→
   235→    let ideDisplay;
   236→    if (!ides || ides.length === 0) {
   237→      ideDisplay = 'your IDE';
   238→    } else if (ides.length === 1) {
   239→      ideDisplay = ideNames[ides[0]] || 'your IDE';
   240→    } else {
   241→      ideDisplay = ides.map((ide) => ideNames[ide] || ide).join(' or ');
   242→    }
   243→
   244→    let noteTitle;
   245→    let noteBody;
   246→
   247→    if (action === 'update') {
   248→      noteTitle = 'Update complete!';
   249→      noteBody = [
   250→        `${chalk.white.bold('What Changed')}`,
   251→        'SKF files and agents have been refreshed.',
   252→        'Your config.yaml and sidecar state are preserved.',
   253→        '',
   254→        `${chalk.white.bold('Next Steps')}`,
   255→        `1. Reload the agent in ${ideDisplay}:`,
   256→        `   ${chalk.cyan(`"Read and activate ${skfFolder}/agents/forger.md"`)}`,
   257→        '2. Run @Ferris SF to re-detect tools if needed',
   258→      ].join('\n');
   259→    } else {
   260→      noteTitle = 'Installation complete!';
   261→      noteBody = [
   262→        `${chalk.white.bold('Get Started')}`,
   263→        `1. Open this folder in ${ideDisplay}`,
   264→        '2. Locate the chat window and type:',
   265→        `   ${chalk.cyan(`"Read and activate ${skfFolder}/agents/forger.md"`)}`,
   266→        '3. Ferris (your Skill Architect) will guide you through',
   267→        '   setting up and forging your first agent skill',
   268→      ].join('\n');
   269→    }
   270→
   271→    note(noteBody, noteTitle);
   272→
   273→    outro(`Agent: Ferris (Skill Architect & Integrity Guardian)\nDocs: https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge`);
   274→  }
   275→}
   276→
   277→module.exports = { UI };
   278→
```

{
  "command": "node -e \"\nconst chalk = require('chalk');\nconst figlet = require('figlet');\n\n// === Final Design: Polished v1.0.0 flagship ===\nconsole.log(chalk.dim('=== Final Design ===\\n'));\n\nconst logo = figlet.textSync('SKF', { font: 'ANSI Shadow' }).trimEnd().split('\\n');\n// Remove trailing empty lines from figlet\nwhile (logo.length && !logo[logo.length - 1].trim()) logo.pop();\n\nconst w = 54;\nconst top    = chalk.cyan('  ╔' + '═'.repeat(w) + '╗');\nconst mid    = chalk.cyan('  ╟' + '─'.repeat(w) + '╢');\nconst bottom = chalk.cyan('  ╚' + '═'.repeat(w) + '╝');\n\nconst row = (content) => {\n  const stripped = content.replace(/\\x1b\\[[0-9;]*m/g, '');\n  const pad = Math.max(0, w - stripped.length - 2);\n  return chalk.cyan('  ║ ') + content + ' '.repeat(pad) + chalk.cyan(' ║');\n};\n\nconsole.log();\nconsole.log(top);\nfor (const line of logo) {\n  console.log(row(chalk.cyan.bold(line.replace(/\\s+$/, ''))));\n}\nconsole.log(mid);\nconsole.log(row(chalk.white.bold('Skill Forge') + chalk.dim(' v1.0.0')));\nconsole.log(row(chalk.dim('Agent Skill Compiler') + '               ' + chalk.cyan('⚒')));\nconsole.log(mid);\nconsole.log(row(chalk.dim('AST-verified · version-pinned · zero hallucination')));\nconsole.log(bottom);\nconsole.log();\n\"",
  "description": "Preview final polished design"
}

     1→/**
     2→ * SKF Installer UI - Banner, prompts, and success message.
     3→ * Uses @clack/prompts for terminal UI.
     4→ */
     5→
     6→const { intro, outro, text, select, multiselect, confirm, note, isCancel, cancel, log } = require('@clack/prompts');
     7→const chalk = require('chalk');
     8→const figlet = require('figlet');
     9→const path = require('node:path');
    10→const fs = require('fs-extra');
    11→const yaml = require('js-yaml');
    12→
    13→const SKF_FOLDER = '_bmad/skf';
    14→
    15→class UI {
    16→  displayBanner() {
    17→    const packageJson = require('../../../package.json');
    18→    const version = packageJson.version;
    19→
    20→    let logoLines;
    21→    try {
    22→      logoLines = figlet
    23→        .textSync('SKF', { font: 'ANSI Shadow' })
    24→        .trimEnd()
    25→        .split('\n');
    26→      // Remove trailing empty lines from figlet output
    27→      while (logoLines.length > 0 && !logoLines.at(-1).trim()) logoLines.pop();
    28→    } catch {
    29→      logoLines = ['  S K F'];
    30→    }
    31→
    32→    const w = 54;
    33→    const top = chalk.cyan('  ╔' + '═'.repeat(w) + '╗');
    34→    const mid = chalk.cyan('  ╟' + '─'.repeat(w) + '╢');
    35→    const bottom = chalk.cyan('  ╚' + '═'.repeat(w) + '╝');
    36→    const row = (content) => {
    37→      // eslint-disable-next-line no-control-regex -- stripping ANSI escape codes for visual width calculation
    38→      const stripped = content.replaceAll(/\u001B\[\d+(?:;\d+)*m/g, '');
    39→      const pad = Math.max(0, w - stripped.length - 2);
    40→      return chalk.cyan('  ║ ') + content + ' '.repeat(pad) + chalk.cyan(' ║');
    41→    };
    42→
    43→    console.log();
    44→    console.log(top);
    45→    for (const line of logoLines) {
    46→      console.log(row(chalk.cyan.bold(line.replace(/\s+$/, ''))));
    47→    }
    48→    console.log(mid);
    49→    console.log(row(chalk.white.bold('Skill Forge') + chalk.dim(` v${version}`)));
    50→    console.log(row(chalk.dim('Agent Skill Compiler') + ' '.repeat(15) + chalk.cyan('⚒')));
    51→    console.log(mid);
    52→    console.log(row(chalk.dim('AST-verified · version-pinned · zero hallucination')));
    53→    console.log(bottom);
    54→    console.log();
    55→
    56→    intro(chalk.cyan('Skill Forge Installer'));
    57→  }
    58→
    59→  async detectInstallation(projectDir) {
    60→    const hasBmadSkf = await fs.pathExists(path.join(projectDir, SKF_FOLDER));
    61→    const hasBmadDir = await fs.pathExists(path.join(projectDir, '_bmad'));
    62→
    63→    if (hasBmadSkf) {
    64→      return { type: 'existing', folder: SKF_FOLDER };
    65→    }
    66→    if (hasBmadDir) {
    67→      return { type: 'bmad-ready', folder: SKF_FOLDER };
    68→    }
    69→    return { type: 'fresh', folder: SKF_FOLDER };
    70→  }
    71→
    72→  async promptInstall() {
    73→    this.displayBanner();
    74→
    75→    const projectDir = process.cwd();
    76→    const defaultProjectName = path.basename(projectDir);
    77→    const detection = await this.detectInstallation(projectDir);
    78→    const skfFolder = detection.folder;
    79→
    80→    log.info(`Target: ${chalk.cyan(projectDir)}`);
    81→
    82→    let action = 'fresh';
    83→
    84→    if (detection.type === 'existing') {
    85→      log.warn(`Found existing installation at ${chalk.white(SKF_FOLDER + '/')}`);
    86→
    87→      const choice = await select({
    88→        message: 'What would you like to do?',
    89→        options: [
    90→          { label: 'Update — Replace SKF files, keep config.yaml', value: 'update' },
    91→          { label: 'Fresh install — Remove everything and start over', value: 'fresh' },
    92→          { label: 'Cancel', value: 'cancel' },
    93→        ],
    94→      });
    95→
    96→      if (isCancel(choice) || choice === 'cancel') {
    97→        cancel('Installation cancelled.');
    98→        return { cancelled: true };
    99→      }
   100→      action = choice;
   101→    } else {
   102→      log.info(`Agents and workflows will be installed in ${chalk.white(skfFolder + '/')}`);
   103→    }
   104→
   105→    if (action === 'update') {
   106→      log.info('Existing config.yaml will be preserved.');
   107→      return {
   108→        projectDir,
   109→        skfFolder,
   110→        _detection: detection,
   111→        _action: action,
   112→        cancelled: false,
   113→      };
   114→    }
   115→
   116→    // Load saved config to pre-populate defaults on fresh reinstall
   117→    const savedConfig = await this.loadSavedConfig(projectDir, skfFolder);
   118→    if (savedConfig) {
   119→      log.info('Previous configuration detected — defaults pre-populated.');
   120→    }
   121→
   122→    const ideOptions = [
   123→      { label: 'Claude Code', value: 'claude-code' },
   124→      { label: 'Cline', value: 'cline' },
   125→      { label: 'Codex', value: 'codex' },
   126→      { label: 'Cursor', value: 'cursor' },
   127→      { label: 'GitHub Copilot', value: 'github-copilot' },
   128→      { label: 'Roo Code', value: 'roo' },
   129→      { label: 'Windsurf', value: 'windsurf' },
   130→      { label: 'Other', value: 'other' },
   131→    ];
   132→
   133→    // Pre-check IDEs: saved config takes priority, then auto-detect from directories
   134→    const savedIdes = savedConfig?.ides || [];
   135→    let initialIdes = [];
   136→    if (savedIdes.length > 0) {
   137→      initialIdes = savedIdes;
   138→    } else {
   139→      const detectedIdes = await this.detectIdes(projectDir);
   140→      if (detectedIdes.length > 0) {
   141→        initialIdes = detectedIdes;
   142→        log.info(`Auto-detected IDEs: ${detectedIdes.join(', ')}`);
   143→      }
   144→    }
   145→
   146→    // Mark initially selected IDEs
   147→    for (const opt of ideOptions) {
   148→      opt.initialSelected = initialIdes.includes(opt.value);
   149→    }
   150→
   151→    // Project name
   152→    const project_name = await text({
   153→      message: 'Project name:',
   154→      initialValue: savedConfig?.project_name || defaultProjectName,
   155→    });
   156→    if (isCancel(project_name)) {
   157→      cancel('Installation cancelled.');
   158→      return { cancelled: true };
   159→    }
   160→
   161→    // Skills output folder
   162→    const skills_output_folder = await text({
   163→      message: 'Where should generated skills be saved?',
   164→      initialValue: savedConfig?.skills_output_folder || 'skills',
   165→    });
   166→    if (isCancel(skills_output_folder)) {
   167→      cancel('Installation cancelled.');
   168→      return { cancelled: true };
   169→    }
   170→
   171→    // Forge data folder
   172→    const forge_data_folder = await text({
   173→      message: 'Where should forge workspace artifacts be stored?',
   174→      initialValue: savedConfig?.forge_data_folder || 'forge-data',
   175→    });
   176→    if (isCancel(forge_data_folder)) {
   177→      cancel('Installation cancelled.');
   178→      return { cancelled: true };
   179→    }
   180→
   181→    // IDE selection
   182→    const ides = await multiselect({
   183→      message: 'Which tools/IDEs are you using?',
   184→      options: ideOptions,
   185→      initialValues: initialIdes,
   186→      required: true,
   187→    });
   188→    if (isCancel(ides)) {
   189→      cancel('Installation cancelled.');
   190→      return { cancelled: true };
   191→    }
   192→
   193→    // Learning material
   194→    const install_learning = await confirm({
   195→      message: 'Install learning & reference material?',
   196→      initialValue: true,
   197→    });
   198→    if (isCancel(install_learning)) {
   199→      cancel('Installation cancelled.');
   200→      return { cancelled: true };
   201→    }
   202→
   203→    return {
   204→      projectDir,
   205→      project_name,
   206→      skills_output_folder,
   207→      forge_data_folder,
   208→      ides,
   209→      install_learning,
   210→      skfFolder,
   211→      _detection: detection,
   212→      _action: action,
   213→      cancelled: false,
   214→    };
   215→  }
   216→
   217→  async detectIdes(projectDir) {
   218→    const markers = {
   219→      'claude-code': ['.claude'],
   220→      cursor: ['.cursor'],
   221→      cline: ['.clinerules'],
   222→      codex: ['.codex'],
   223→      'github-copilot': ['.github/copilot-instructions.md', '.github/prompts'],
   224→      roo: ['.roo', '.roomodes'],
   225→      windsurf: ['.windsurf'],
   226→    };
   227→
   228→    const detected = [];
   229→    for (const [ide, paths] of Object.entries(markers)) {
   230→      for (const p of paths) {
   231→        if (await fs.pathExists(path.join(projectDir, p))) {
   232→          detected.push(ide);
   233→          break;
   234→        }
   235→      }
   236→    }
   237→    return detected;
   238→  }
   239→
   240→  async loadSavedConfig(projectDir, skfFolder) {
   241→    const configPath = path.join(projectDir, skfFolder, 'config.yaml');
   242→    try {
   243→      if (await fs.pathExists(configPath)) {
   244→        const content = await fs.readFile(configPath, 'utf8');
   245→        return yaml.load(content) || null;
   246→      }
   247→    } catch {
   248→      // ignore parse errors
   249→    }
   250→    return null;
   251→  }
   252→
   253→  displaySuccess(skfFolder, ides = [], action = 'fresh') {
   254→    const ideNames = {
   255→      'claude-code': 'Claude Code',
   256→      cline: 'Cline',
   257→      codex: 'Codex',
   258→      cursor: 'Cursor',
   259→      'github-copilot': 'GitHub Copilot',
   260→      roo: 'Roo Code',
   261→      windsurf: 'Windsurf',
   262→      other: 'your IDE',
   263→    };
   264→
   265→    let ideDisplay;
   266→    if (!ides || ides.length === 0) {
   267→      ideDisplay = 'your IDE';
   268→    } else if (ides.length === 1) {
   269→      ideDisplay = ideNames[ides[0]] || 'your IDE';
   270→    } else {
   271→      ideDisplay = ides.map((ide) => ideNames[ide] || ide).join(' or ');
   272→    }
   273→
   274→    let noteTitle;
   275→    let noteBody;
   276→
   277→    if (action === 'update') {
   278→      noteTitle = 'Update complete!';
   279→      noteBody = [
   280→        `${chalk.white.bold('What Changed')}`,
   281→        'SKF files and agents have been refreshed.',
   282→        'Your config.yaml and sidecar state are preserved.',
   283→        '',
   284→        `${chalk.white.bold('Next Steps')}`,
   285→        `1. Reload the agent in ${ideDisplay}:`,
   286→        `   ${chalk.cyan(`"Read and activate ${skfFolder}/agents/forger.md"`)}`,
   287→        '2. Run @Ferris SF to re-detect tools if needed',
   288→      ].join('\n');
   289→    } else {
   290→      noteTitle = 'Installation complete!';
   291→      noteBody = [
   292→        `${chalk.white.bold('Get Started')}`,
   293→        `1. Open this folder in ${ideDisplay}`,
   294→        '2. Locate the chat window and type:',
   295→        `   ${chalk.cyan(`"Read and activate ${skfFolder}/agents/forger.md"`)}`,
   296→        '3. Ferris (your Skill Architect) will guide you through',
   297→        '   setting up and forging your first agent skill',
   298→      ].join('\n');
   299→    }
   300→
   301→    note(noteBody, noteTitle);
   302→
   303→    outro(`Agent: Ferris (Skill Architect & Integrity Guardian)\nDocs: https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge`);
   304→  }
   305→}
   306→
   307→module.exports = { UI };
   308→

const savedIdes = savedConfig?.ides || [];\n    let initialIdes = [];\n    if (savedIdes.length > 0) {\n      initialIdes = savedIdes;\n    } else {\n      const detectedIdes = await this.detectIdes(projectDir);\n      if (detectedIdes.length > 0) {\n        initialIdes = detectedIdes;\n        log.info(`Auto-detected IDEs: ${brand.gold(detectedIdes.join(', '))}`);\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Mark initially selected IDEs\n    for (const opt of ideOptions) {\n      opt.initialSelected = initialIdes.includes(opt.value);\n    }\n\n    // Project name\n    const project_name = cancelled.');\n      return { cancelled: true };\n    }\n\n    // Skills output folder\n    const skills_output_folder = await text({\n      message: 'Where should generated skills be saved?',\n cancel('Installation cancelled.');\n      return { cancelled: true };\n    }\n\n    // Learning material\n    const install_learning = await confirm({\n      message: 'Install learning & reference material?',\n      initialValue: true,\n    });\n    if (isCancel(install_learning)) {\n      cancel('Installation cancelled.');\n      return { cancelled: true };\n    }\n\n    return {\n      projectDir,\n      project_name,\n      skills_output_folder,\n      forge_data_folder,\n      ides,\n      install_learning,\n      skfFolder,\n      _detection: detection,\n      _action: action,\n      cancelled: false,\n    };\n  }\n\n  async detectIdes(projectDir) {\n    const markers = {\n      'claude-code': ['.claude'],\n      cursor: ['.cursor'],\n      cline: ['.clinerules'],\n      codex: ['.codex'],\n      'github-copilot': ['.github/copilot-instructions.md', '.github/prompts'],\n      roo: ['.roo', '.roomodes'],\n      windsurf: ['.windsurf'],\n    };\n\n    const detected = [];\n    for (const [ide, paths] of Object.entries(markers)) {\n      for (const p of paths) {\n        if (await fs.pathExists(path.join(projectDir, p))) {\n          detected.push(ide);\n    if (await fs.pathExists(configPath)) {\n        const content = await fs.readFile(configPath, 'utf8');\n        return yaml.load(content) || null;\n      }\n    } catch {\n      // ignore parse errors\n    }\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  displaySuccess(skfFolder, ides = [], action = 'fresh') {\n    const ideNames = {\n      'claude-code': 'Claude Code',\n      cline: 'Cline',\n      codex: 'Codex',\n      cursor: 'Cursor',\n      'github-copilot': 'GitHub Copilot',\n      roo: 'Roo Code',\n      windsurf: 'Windsurf',\n      other: 'your IDE',\n    };\n\n    let ideDisplay;\n    if (!ides || ides.length === 0) {\n      ideDisplay = 'your IDE';\n    } else if (ides.length === 1) {\n      ideDisplay = ideNames[ides[0]] || 'your IDE';\n    } else {\n      ideDisplay = ides.map((ide) => ideNames[ide] || ide).join(' or ');\n    }\n\n    let noteTitle;\n    let noteBody;\n\n    if (action === 'update') {\n      noteTitle = brand.amber.bold('Update complete!');\n      noteBody = [\n        `${chalk.white.bold('What Changed')}`,\n        'SKF files and agents have been refreshed.',\n        'Your config.yaml and sidecar state are preserved.',\n        '',\n        `${chalk.white.bold('Next Steps')}`,\n        `1. Reload the agent in ${ideDisplay}:`,\n        `   ${brand.gold(`\"Read and activate ${skfFolder}/agents/forger.md\"`)}`,\n        '2. Run @Ferris SF to re-detect tools if needed',\n      ].join('\\n');\n    } else {\n      noteTitle = brand.amber.bold('Installation complete!');\n      noteBody = [\n        `${chalk.white.bold('Get Started')}`,\n        `1. Open this folder in ${ideDisplay}`,\n        '2. Locate the chat window and type:',\n        `   ${brand.gold(`\"Read and activate ${skfFolder}/agents/forger.md\"`)}`,\n        '3. Ferris (your Skill Architect) will guide you through',\n        '   setting up and forging your first agent skill',\n      ].join('\\n');\n    }\n\n    note(noteBody, noteTitle);\n\n    outro(\n      `${brand.spark('⚒')}  Agent: ${chalk.white('Ferris')} ${chalk.dim('(Skill Architect & Integrity Guardian)')}\\n${brand.dark('⚡')} Docs: ${brand.amber('https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge')}`,\n    );\n  }\n}\n\nmodule.exports = { UI };\n"
}

     1→/**
     2→ * SKF Status Command
     3→ * Shows installation state, version, tier, configured IDEs, and sidecar status.
     4→ */
     5→
     6→const chalk = require('chalk');
     7→const path = require('node:path');
     8→const fs = require('fs-extra');
     9→const yaml = require('js-yaml');
    10→const { readManifest } = require('../lib/manifest');
    11→
    12→const SKF_FOLDER = '_bmad/skf';
    13→const SIDECAR_FOLDER = '_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar';
    14→
    15→const IDE_NAMES = {
    16→  'claude-code': 'Claude Code',
    17→  cline: 'Cline',
    18→  codex: 'Codex',
    19→  cursor: 'Cursor',
    20→  'github-copilot': 'GitHub Copilot',
    21→  roo: 'Roo Code',
    22→  windsurf: 'Windsurf',
    23→  other: 'Other',
    24→};
    25→
    26→async function readYaml(filePath) {
    27→  try {
    28→    const content = await fs.readFile(filePath, 'utf8');
    29→    return yaml.load(content) || {};
    30→  } catch {
    31→    return null;
    32→  }
    33→}
    34→
    35→async function getStatus(projectDir) {
    36→  const skfDir = path.join(projectDir, SKF_FOLDER);
    37→  const sidecarDir = path.join(projectDir, SIDECAR_FOLDER);
    38→
    39→  const installed = await fs.pathExists(skfDir);
    40→  if (!installed) {
    41→    return { installed: false };
    42→  }
    43→
    44→  // Read config
    45→  const config = await readYaml(path.join(skfDir, 'config.yaml'));
    46→
    47→  // Read forge tier
    48→  const forgeTier = await readYaml(path.join(sidecarDir, 'forge-tier.yaml'));
    49→
    50→  // Read preferences
    51→  const preferences = await readYaml(path.join(sidecarDir, 'preferences.yaml'));
    52→
    53→  // Check agent file
    54→  const agentFile = path.join(skfDir, 'agents', 'forger.md');
    55→  const agentCompiled = await fs.pathExists(agentFile);
    56→
    57→  // Count workflows
    58→  const workflowsDir = path.join(skfDir, 'workflows', 'skillforge');
    59→  let workflowCount = 0;
    60→  if (await fs.pathExists(workflowsDir)) {
    61→    const entries = await fs.readdir(workflowsDir);
    62→    for (const entry of entries) {
    63→      const wfPath = path.join(workflowsDir, entry, 'workflow.md');
    64→      if (await fs.pathExists(wfPath)) {
    65→        workflowCount++;
    66→      }
    67→    }
    68→  }
    69→
    70→  // Check output folders
    71→  const skillsFolder = config?.skills_output_folder || 'skills';
    72→  const forgeDataFolder = config?.forge_data_folder || 'forge-data';
    73→  const skillsFolderExists = await fs.pathExists(path.join(projectDir, skillsFolder));
    74→  const forgeDataFolderExists = await fs.pathExists(path.join(projectDir, forgeDataFolder));
    75→
    76→  // Check sidecar state
    77→  const sidecarExists = await fs.pathExists(sidecarDir);
    78→  const tierDetected = forgeTier?.tier != null;
    79→
    80→  // Read manifest
    81→  const manifest = await readManifest(projectDir);
    82→
    83→  return {
    84→    installed: true,
    85→    config,
    86→    forgeTier,
    87→    preferences,
    88→    agentCompiled,
    89→    workflowCount,
    90→    skillsFolder,
    91→    skillsFolderExists,
    92→    forgeDataFolder,
    93→    forgeDataFolderExists,
    94→    sidecarExists,
    95→    tierDetected,
    96→    manifest,
    97→  };
    98→}
    99→
   100→function displayStatus(status, version) {
   101→  console.log('');
   102→  console.log(chalk.cyan.bold('  Skill Forge — Status'));
   103→  console.log(chalk.dim(`  v${version}`));
   104→  console.log('');
   105→
   106→  if (!status.installed) {
   107→    console.log(chalk.yellow('  Not installed.'));
   108→    console.log(chalk.dim('  Run: npx bmad-module-skill-forge install'));
   109→    console.log('');
   110→    return;
   111→  }
   112→
   113→  const config = status.config || {};
   114→
   115→  // Installation
   116→  const manifest = status.manifest;
   117→  console.log(chalk.white.bold('  Installation'));
   118→  console.log(`    Project:      ${chalk.cyan(config.project_name || '(unknown)')}`);
   119→  console.log(`    SKF folder:   ${chalk.dim(SKF_FOLDER + '/')}`);
   120→  console.log(`    Agent:        ${status.agentCompiled ? chalk.green('compiled') : chalk.yellow('not compiled')}`);
   121→  console.log(`    Workflows:    ${chalk.white(status.workflowCount)}`);
   122→  if (manifest) {
   123→    console.log(
   124→      `    Installed:    ${chalk.dim(manifest.installed_at ? new Date(manifest.installed_at).toLocaleDateString() : '(unknown)')}`,
   125→    );
   126→    console.log(`    Manifest:     ${chalk.green('present')}`);
   127→  } else {
   128→    console.log(`    Manifest:     ${chalk.yellow('missing')} ${chalk.dim('(reinstall to generate)')}`);
   129→  }
   130→  console.log('');
   131→
   132→  // IDEs
   133→  const ides = config.ides || [];
   134→  console.log(chalk.white.bold('  IDEs'));
   135→  if (ides.length > 0) {
   136→    for (const ide of ides) {
   137→      console.log(`    ${chalk.green('●')} ${IDE_NAMES[ide] || ide}`);
   138→    }
   139→  } else {
   140→    console.log(chalk.dim('    None configured'));
   141→  }
   142→  console.log('');
   143→
   144→  // Forge Tier
   145→  const ft = status.forgeTier || {};
   146→  const tools = ft.tools || {};
   147→  console.log(chalk.white.bold('  Forge Tier'));
   148→  if (status.tierDetected) {
   149→    const tierColors = { Quick: chalk.yellow, Forge: chalk.cyan, Deep: chalk.magenta };
   150→    const tierColor = tierColors[ft.tier] || chalk.white;
   151→    console.log(`    Tier:         ${tierColor(ft.tier)}`);
   152→    console.log(`    Detected:     ${chalk.dim(ft.tier_detected_at || '(unknown)')}`);
   153→  } else {
   154→    console.log(chalk.dim('    Not detected yet — run @Ferris SF'));
   155→  }
   156→  console.log(`    ast-grep:     ${formatTool(tools.ast_grep)}`);
   157→  console.log(`    gh CLI:       ${formatTool(tools.gh_cli)}`);
   158→  console.log(`    QMD:          ${formatTool(tools.qmd)}`);
   159→  console.log('');
   160→
   161→  // Output Folders
   162→  console.log(chalk.white.bold('  Output Folders'));
   163→  console.log(`    Skills:       ${status.skillsFolder}/ ${status.skillsFolderExists ? chalk.green('✓') : chalk.yellow('missing')}`);
   164→  console.log(`    Forge data:   ${status.forgeDataFolder}/ ${status.forgeDataFolderExists ? chalk.green('✓') : chalk.yellow('missing')}`);
   165→  console.log('');
   166→
   167→  // Sidecar
   168→  console.log(chalk.white.bold('  Sidecar'));
   169→  console.log(`    State:        ${status.sidecarExists ? chalk.green('initialized') : chalk.yellow('missing')}`);
   170→  console.log(`    Location:     ${chalk.dim(SIDECAR_FOLDER + '/')}`);
   171→  console.log('');
   172→}
   173→
   174→function formatTool(value) {
   175→  if (value === true || value === 'available') return chalk.green('available');
   176→  if (value == null) return chalk.dim('not detected');
   177→  return chalk.yellow(String(value));
   178→}
   179→
   180→module.exports = {
   181→  command: 'status',
   182→  description: 'Show SKF installation state, version, tier, and configuration',
   183→  options: [],
   184→  action: async () => {
   185→    try {
   186→      const projectDir = process.cwd();
   187→      const packageJson = require('../../../package.json');
   188→      const status = await getStatus(projectDir);
   189→      displayStatus(status, packageJson.version);
   190→    } catch (error) {
   191→      console.error(chalk.red('\nFailed to read status:'), error.message);
   192→      process.exit(1);
   193→    }
   194→  },
   195→};
   196→

     1→/**
     2→ * SKF Quick Update Command
     3→ * Replaces SKF files and recompiles agents without re-prompting.
     4→ * Preserves config.yaml and sidecar state.
     5→ */
     6→
     7→const chalk = require('chalk');
     8→const path = require('node:path');
     9→const fs = require('fs-extra');
    10→const yaml = require('js-yaml');
    11→const { Installer } = require('../lib/installer');
    12→const { UI } = require('../lib/ui');
    13→
    14→const SKF_FOLDER = '_bmad/skf';
    15→
    16→module.exports = {
    17→  command: 'update',
    18→  description: 'Update SKF files and recompile agents (preserves config and sidecar)',
    19→  options: [],
    20→  action: async () => {
    21→    try {
    22→      const projectDir = process.cwd();
    23→      const skfDir = path.join(projectDir, SKF_FOLDER);
    24→
    25→      if (!(await fs.pathExists(skfDir))) {
    26→        console.log(chalk.yellow('\n  SKF is not installed in this directory.'));
    27→        console.log(chalk.dim('  Run: npx bmad-module-skill-forge install\n'));
    28→        process.exit(0);
    29→        return;
    30→      }
    31→
    32→      console.log('');
    33→      console.log(chalk.cyan.bold('  Skill Forge — Quick Update'));
    34→      console.log(chalk.dim('  Replacing SKF files, preserving config and sidecar.\n'));
    35→
    36→      const installer = new Installer();
    37→      const result = await installer.install({
    38→        projectDir,
    39→        skfFolder: SKF_FOLDER,
    40→        _action: 'update',
    41→      });
    42→
    43→      if (result && result.success) {
    44→        // Read config to get IDEs for post-update notes
    45→        let ides = [];
    46→        try {
    47→          const configContent = await fs.readFile(path.join(skfDir, 'config.yaml'), 'utf8');
    48→          const config = yaml.load(configContent);
    49→          ides = config?.ides || [];
    50→        } catch {
    51→          /* use empty */
    52→        }
    53→        const ui = new UI();
    54→        ui.displaySuccess(SKF_FOLDER, ides, 'update');
    55→      }
    56→
    57→      process.exit(0);
    58→    } catch (error) {
    59→      console.error(chalk.red('\nUpdate failed:'), error.message);
    60→      process.exit(1);
    61→    }
    62→  },
    63→};
    64→

     1→/**
     2→ * SKF Version Check
     3→ * Async, non-blocking check against the npm registry.
     4→ * Returns a promise that resolves to an update notice string (or null).
     5→ */
     6→
     7→const https = require('node:https');
     8→const chalk = require('chalk');
     9→
    10→const PACKAGE_NAME = 'bmad-module-skill-forge';
    11→const REGISTRY_URL = `https://registry.npmjs.org/${PACKAGE_NAME}/latest`;
    12→const TIMEOUT_MS = 3000;
    13→
    14→function fetchLatestVersion() {
    15→  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    16→    const req = https.get(REGISTRY_URL, { timeout: TIMEOUT_MS }, (res) => {
    17→      if (res.statusCode !== 200) {
    18→        resolve(null);
    19→        res.resume();
    20→        return;
    21→      }
    22→
    23→      let data = '';
    24→      res.on('data', (chunk) => {
    25→        data += chunk;
    26→      });
    27→      res.on('end', () => {
    28→        try {
    29→          const json = JSON.parse(data);
    30→          resolve(json.version || null);
    31→        } catch {
    32→          resolve(null);
    33→        }
    34→      });
    35→    });
    36→
    37→    req.on('error', () => resolve(null));
    38→    req.on('timeout', () => {
    39→      req.destroy();
    40→      resolve(null);
    41→    });
    42→  });
    43→}
    44→
    45→function compareVersions(current, latest) {
    46→  const parse = (v) => v.replace(/^v/, '').split('.').map(Number);
    47→  const [cMajor, cMinor, cPatch] = parse(current);
    48→  const [lMajor, lMinor, lPatch] = parse(latest);
    49→
    50→  if (lMajor > cMajor) return true;
    51→  if (lMajor === cMajor && lMinor > cMinor) return true;
    52→  if (lMajor === cMajor && lMinor === cMinor && lPatch > cPatch) return true;
    53→  return false;
    54→}
    55→
    56→/**
    57→ * Start an async version check. Call the returned function after your
    58→ * command finishes to print the update notice (if any).
    59→ */
    60→function startVersionCheck(currentVersion) {
    61→  const checkPromise = fetchLatestVersion().then((latestVersion) => {
    62→    if (!latestVersion || !compareVersions(currentVersion, latestVersion)) {
    63→      return null;
    64→    }
    65→    return (
    66→      '\n' +
    67→      chalk.yellow(`  Update available: ${chalk.dim(currentVersion)} → ${chalk.green(latestVersion)}`) +
    68→      '\n' +
    69→      chalk.dim(`  Run: npx bmad-module-skill-forge@latest install`) +
    70→      '\n'
    71→    );
    72→  });
    73→
    74→  return async function printIfReady() {
    75→    try {
    76→      const notice = await checkPromise;
    77→      if (notice) {
    78→        process.stderr.write(notice);
    79→      }
    80→    } catch {
    81→      // Never block or fail the CLI for a version check
    82→    }
    83→  };
    84→}
    85→
    86→module.exports = { startVersionCheck, compareVersions };
    87→
```

Reviewing `git diff origin/main..dev` across the full set of changes.

## Summary

I reviewed all changed and related files. The changes include: the README star badge and CTA, the cognee doc URL fix, the Vercel research link fix, the ADR-L v2 context-snippet format enhancement (description + refs index), and the docs-only pipeline support across lifecycle workflows. Here are the high-confidence findings:

---

## Critical

### 1. Broken cross-reference: step-03-re-extract.md uses a source-tree path that doesn't exist at runtime

**Confidence: 100**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`, line 116

The step contains this instruction:

```
Follow the AST Extraction Protocol from `src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`.
```

This path is a repo source-tree path. At runtime, the installer copies all of `src/workflows/` into `_bmad/skf/workflows/skillforge/` (confirmed in `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/tools/cli/lib/installer.js`, lines 175-178). The file lands at:

```
_bmad/skf/workflows/skillforge/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md
```

The `src/` prefix does not exist in the installed layout. The path as written is unresolvable at runtime.

By contrast, `create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` handles this correctly — it declares a frontmatter variable `extractionPatternsData: '../data/extraction-patterns.md'` (a relative path that resolves to the sibling `data/` directory) and then references it as `{extractionPatternsData}` in the body. The update-skill step has no such frontmatter variable and instead hardcodes the broken absolute source path in prose.

**Fix:** Either add a frontmatter variable to `step-03-re-extract.md` that uses a correct relative path pointing to the create-skill data directory:

```yaml
extractionPatternsData: '../../create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md'
```

and reference it as `{extractionPatternsData}`, or rewrite the prose reference to use the relative path directly. Note that the relative path `../../create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md` is relative to `update-skill/steps-c/`, which resolves correctly to `update-skill/../../create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md` = `create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md` inside the `skillforge/` subtree.

---

## Important

### 2. ADR-L v2 token target is inconsistent across files after the format upgrade

**Confidence: 92**

The ADR-L v2 commit upgraded the context-snippet format from a strict two-line, ~30 token format to a multi-line format with description + exports + refs. The canonical format specification was updated in `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/export-skill/data/snippet-format.md` to ~50-80 tokens. However, numerous other files still reference the old ~30-token target or still call the format "two-line":

Files still saying **"~30 tokens"** that should now say **"~50-80 tokens"**:
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/export-skill/workflow-plan-export-skill.md` — lines 66, 177, 238, 295 (also still says "two-line per skill")
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md` — lines 13, 48 (the step goal and context-boundaries say ~30, but the actual generation logic at line 103 already says ~50-80)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-05-token-report.md` — line 86 (benchmark still reads "~30 tokens per skill")
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/workflow-plan-quick-skill.md` — lines 159, 280
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md` — line 52 (still shows "two-line format")
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md` — lines 13, 61, 102 (step goal and generation say "two-line" and ~30 tokens)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-05-validate.md` — line 110 (validation criterion checks for ≤30 tokens — this will incorrectly flag valid v2 snippets as failures)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/data/stack-skill-template.md` — line 54 (still "two-line")
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md` — line 103
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/workflow-plan-create-stack-skill.md` — lines 182, 338
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-08-validate.md` — line 142 (still says "two-line format")

The most operationally damaging is `quick-skill/steps-c/step-05-validate.md` line 110, where the validation checklist literally reads `"~30 tokens or less"` — this means any v2 snippet with description + exports + refs (which will typically be 50-80 tokens) will be flagged as a validation failure in the Quick Skill pipeline.

---

### 3. ast-grep MCP server mention in README has no corresponding coverage in docs/getting-started.md prerequisites table

**Confidence: 85**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/README.md`, line 163

The README contains this recommendation:

> If your IDE supports MCP servers, install the ast-grep MCP server alongside the CLI. SKF's extraction protocol prefers the MCP tool for compact, memory-efficient AST queries...

The `docs/getting-started.md` Prerequisites table at lines 58-64 lists `gh`, `ast-grep`, and `qmd` but makes no mention of the ast-grep MCP server. Given that the extraction protocol explicitly prefers the MCP tool over the CLI (as documented in `src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`), users reading only the docs site will be missing guidance on a recommended setup step.

The README correctly covers this. The docs site prerequisites table does not, creating an inconsistency between the two entry points for new users.

**Fix:** Add a row to the prerequisites table in `docs/getting-started.md`:

```
| ast-grep MCP server (optional, recommended) | Forge + Deep modes | https://github.com/ast-grep/ast-grep-mcp |
```

id: toolu_01XPXXsFuZ9GEqsr97dZCy8U
```
     1→# Context Snippet Format (ADR-L v2)
     2→
     3→## Format Rules
     4→
     5→- Consistent multi-line format per skill — no adaptive switching
     6→- ~50-80 tokens per skill target (description + exports + optional refs)
     7→- T1-now content only (AST-current, no annotations)
     8→- Description line is the WHEN-TO-USE signal — compressed from SKILL.md frontmatter
     9→- Refs line is the WHERE-TO-LOOK signal — only when references/ exists
    10→
    11→## Single Skill Snippet Template
    12→
    13→```markdown
    14→{skill-name} → skills/{skill-name}/
    15→  {compressed-description — ~15 words from SKILL.md frontmatter description}
    16→  exports: {export-1}, {export-2}, {export-3}, {export-4}, {export-5}
    17→  refs: {ref-1}, {ref-2}, {ref-3}
    18→```
    19→
    20→- **Line 1:** Name + path pointer (always present)
    21→- **Line 2:** Compressed description from SKILL.md frontmatter `description:` field (~15 words, trigger-optimized)
    22→- **Line 3:** Top exports from metadata.json `exports` array (up to 10 for Deep tier, 5 otherwise)
    23→- **Line 4:** Reference file names without extensions (only when `references/` directory exists and contains files)
    24→
    25→## Stack Skill Snippet Template
    26→
    27→```markdown
    28→{project}-stack → skills/{project}-stack/
    29→  {compressed-description}
    30→  stack: {dep-1}@{v1}, {dep-2}@{v2}, {dep-3}@{v3}
    31→  integrations: {pattern-1}, {pattern-2}, {pattern-3}
    32→```
    33→
    34→## Rules
    35→
    36→- **description**: Compress SKILL.md frontmatter `description:` to ~15 words. Focus on WHAT it does and WHEN to use it.
    37→- **exports**: Top exports from metadata.json `exports` array (by order as listed)
    38→- **refs**: File names from `references/` directory, without `.md` extension, sorted. Omit line if no references/ exists.
    39→- **stack**: Component versions from metadata.json `components` for stack skills
    40→- **integrations**: Co-import patterns from metadata.json `integrations` for stack skills
    41→- If fewer exports than the limit, list all available
    42→- If no exports data available, omit the exports line
    43→- Skill path is relative to project root
    44→
```
