```
Here is the complete pre-release audit report for version 1.0.0 of bmad-module-skill-forge.

---

# Pre-Release Audit Report — bmad-module-skill-forge v1.0.0

---

## BLOCKER

### 1. `package.json` version is still `0.2.0` — must be bumped to `1.0.0` before release

Confidence: 100

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/package.json`, line 4

The `version` field is `"0.2.0"`. This is the only source of truth for what gets published to npm. The `0.2.0` references found in `package-lock.json` are expected by-products and will auto-update when you run `npm version 1.0.0`. There are zero stale `0.2.0` references in any doc, workflow, or config file — only in `package.json` and `package-lock.json` as expected.

Fix: `npm version 1.0.0` (or manually set `"version": "1.0.0"` and regenerate the lockfile).

---

### 2. `publish.yaml` uses Node.js 20 — contradicts `engines: node >= 22` requirement

Confidence: 95

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/.github/workflows/publish.yaml`, line 22

The publish workflow pins `node-version: "20"` while `package.json` declares `"engines": { "node": ">=22.0.0" }` and `.nvmrc` contains `22`. This means the actual npm publish runs on Node 20, which the package explicitly declares as unsupported. If any publish-time code requires Node 22 features (e.g., native `fetch`, certain `fs` APIs), this will silently fail or produce a broken published artifact. All other workflows (`quality.yaml`, `manual-release.yaml`) correctly use `.nvmrc`.

Fix: Change line 22 in `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/.github/workflows/publish.yaml` from `node-version: "20"` to `node-version-file: ".nvmrc"`.

---

### 3. `manual-release.yaml` calls `npm run validate` — this script does not exist

Confidence: 100

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/.github/workflows/manual-release.yaml`, line 44

The release workflow runs `npm run validate` as a pre-release gate. This script is not defined in `package.json` (the defined scripts are `validate:schemas`, `test:schemas`, `test:install`, `test:knowledge`, `test:cli`). Running `npm run validate` will fail with `npm error Missing script: "validate"`, making the entire manual-release workflow non-functional. This is the workflow used to cut the 1.0.0 release.

Fix: Either add `"validate": "npm run validate:schemas"` to the `scripts` in `package.json`, or change line 44 in `manual-release.yaml` to a script that exists, such as `npm run validate:schemas && npm run test:schemas && npm run test:install`.

---

## IMPORTANT

### 4. `CHANGELOG.md` exists but is empty (1-line file)

Confidence: 90

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/CHANGELOG.md`

The file exists but contains only one blank line. For a 1.0.0 release, this is a significant gap: users, npm auditors, and integrators expect a changelog documenting what is in the release. The `manual-release.yaml` workflow does generate GitHub Release notes dynamically from commit history, but that content never lands in `CHANGELOG.md`. This means npm package consumers who check the file in the package find it empty.

Fix: Populate `CHANGELOG.md` with at least a `## [1.0.0]` section summarizing the initial release scope before publishing.

---

### 5. `docs/architecture.md` references `vercel-labs/skills` — this repository may not exist

Confidence: 80

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/docs/architecture.md`, line 329

The architecture page lists `Compatible with [agentskills/agentskills](https://github.com/agentskills/agentskills) and [vercel-labs/skills](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills)`. The `vercel-labs/skills` repository is not a well-known public repo (Vercel's agent skills work was under `vercel-labs/ai` or similar). If this URL 404s, it undermines credibility in documentation that explicitly emphasizes provenance and zero hallucination tolerance.

Fix: Verify the `vercel-labs/skills` URL is live before release. If it 404s, either remove the reference or correct the URL to the actual repository.

---

### 6. `docs.yaml` also uses Node 20

Confidence: 85

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/.github/workflows/docs.yaml`, line 36

Same issue as finding #2. The docs build runs on Node 20 while the project requires Node 22. If any documentation build tooling (Astro, build-docs.js) depends on Node 22 behavior, this will silently produce different output than a local build.

Fix: Change `node-version: "20"` to `node-version-file: ".nvmrc"` on line 36.

---

## NICE-TO-HAVE

### 7. `CHANGELOG.md` is excluded from npm package by `.npmignore` — but it's currently empty anyway

Confidence: N/A (informational)

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/.npmignore`, line 7

The pattern `/*.md` with `!/README.md` correctly excludes `CHANGELOG.md` from the npm package. This is intentional and fine. Noting for completeness — if you later want the CHANGELOG included in the npm package, you'd need to add `!/CHANGELOG.md`.

---

## CONFIRMED CLEAN

The following items from the audit checklist are clean — no action needed:

**Stale format references:** Zero matches for `~30 token`, `two-line format`, or `two-line` anywhere in `src/`. The fix is complete.

**Medium articles:** Zero matches for `~30 token`, `two-line format`, or `two-line` in `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/medium-article-skf.md` or `medium-article-deep-tier.md`.

**Security scan:** No `.env` files with real secrets, no API keys or credentials committed. Workflow secrets are all properly parameterized via `${{ secrets.* }}`.

**`nextStepFile` cross-reference integrity:** Every step file's `nextStepFile` frontmatter value resolves to an actual file that exists in the same `steps-c/` directory. All chain terminations are correctly marked with no `nextStepFile` or explicit "FINAL step" language. No broken references found.

**npm publish metadata:** `package.json` has all required fields: `name`, `description`, `main`, `bin`, `keywords`, `license`, `repository`, `publishConfig: { access: public }`. The `files` field is not used but `.npmignore` is present and correctly configured to exclude dev files (`test/`, `.github/`, `website/`, `_bmad/`, `_bmad-output/`, `node_modules/`, build tooling, IDE config).

**Version references in docs/comments:** `0.2.0` appears only in `package.json` (line 4) and `package-lock.json` — nowhere in docs, workflow files, source files, or knowledge base. One `npm version 1.0.0` command cleans both.

**README accuracy:** All 10 workflows are present and correctly described (SF, AN, BS, CS, QS, SS, US, AS, TS, EX). The progressive capability model table matches the `src/workflows/setup-forge/data/tier-rules.md` and `docs/architecture.md` definitions. The Ferris operating modes table correctly maps all 10 workflows to their modes.

**Broken links in README/docs:** All external URLs in README.md (`github.com/armelhbobdad/...`, `agentskills.io`, `cli.github.com`, `ast-grep/ast-grep`, `thedaviddias/skill-check`, `tessl.io`, `bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD`) appear to be real, live projects. The one exception to verify is `vercel-labs/skills` (finding #5 above).

---

## Summary

| Severity | Count | Items |
|---|---|---|
| Blocker | 3 | version bump, Node version in publish.yaml, missing `validate` npm script |
| Important | 2 | empty CHANGELOG.md, potential dead link to vercel-labs/skills |
| Nice-to-have | 0 | — |

The three blockers must be resolved before cutting the release tag. The most critical sequencing issue is finding #3: the `manual-release.yaml` workflow will fail immediately at the test step because `npm run validate` is not defined, meaning the automated release process cannot complete as-is.
```

   160→          VERSION="${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}"
   161→          if [[ "$VERSION" == *"alpha"* ]] || [[ "$VERSION" == *"beta"* ]]; then
   162→            echo "Publishing prerelease version with --tag alpha"
   163→            npm publish --tag alpha
   164→          else
   165→            echo "Publishing stable version with --tag latest"
   166→            npm publish --tag latest
   167→          fi
   168→
   169→      - name: Create GitHub Release
   170→        uses: softprops/action-gh-release@v2
   171→        with:
   172→          tag_name: v${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}
   173→          name: "Skill Forge (SKF) v${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}"
   174→          body: |
   175→            ${{ steps.release_notes.outputs.RELEASE_NOTES }}
   176→          draft: false
   177→          prerelease: ${{ contains(steps.version.outputs.new_version, 'alpha') || contains(steps.version.outputs.new_version, 'beta') }}
   178→
   179→      - name: Summary
   180→        run: |
   181→          echo "## 🎉 Successfully released v${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}!" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   182→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   183→          echo "### 📦 Distribution" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   184→          echo "- **NPM**: Published with @latest tag" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   185→          echo "- **GitHub Release**: https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/releases/tag/v${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   186→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   187→          echo "### ✅ Installation" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   188→          echo "\`\`\`bash" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   189→          echo "npx bmad-module-skill-forge@${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }} install" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   190→          echo "\`\`\`" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   191→

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/CHANGELOG.md",
  "content": "# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.\n\n## [1.0.0] - 2026-03-15\n\n### Initial Release\n\nSkill Forge (SKF) — an agent skill compiler that transforms code repositories, documentation, and developer discourse into [agentskills.io](https://agentskills.io)-compliant agent skills with AST-backed provenance.\n\n### Highlights\n\n- **1 agent** — Ferris (Skill Architect & Integrity Guardian) with 4 workflow-driven modes\n- **10 workflows** — full lifecycle from source analysis to ecosystem-ready export\n- **Progressive capability model** — Quick (gh), Forge (+ast-grep), Deep (+QMD)\n- **Zero hallucination tolerance** — every instruction traces to source code with provenance citations\n- **Dual-output strategy** — active skills (SKILL.md) + passive context (context-snippet.md) in ADR-L v2 format\n- **CLI installer** — `npx bmad-module-skill-forge install` with IDE command generation for 7 IDEs\n- **10 knowledge fragments** — curated cross-cutting principles loaded just-in-time by workflows\n\n### Workflows\n\n| Trigger | Name | Purpose |\n|---------|------|---------|\n| SF | Setup Forge | Initialize forge environment, detect tools, set tier |\n| AN | Analyze Source | Discover what to skill in a large repo |\n| BS | Brief Skill | Design a skill scope through guided discovery |\n| CS | Create Skill | Compile a skill from brief with AST extraction |\n| QS | Quick Skill | Fast skill from package name or GitHub URL |\n| SS | Stack Skill | Consolidated project stack skill with integration patterns |\n| US | Update Skill | Smart regeneration preserving manual sections |\n| AS | Audit Skill | Drift detection between skill and current source |\n| TS | Test Skill | Cognitive completeness verification |\n| EX | Export Skill | Package for distribution, inject into CLAUDE.md |\n\n### Confidence Tiers\n\n- **T1** — AST-verified structural truth (Forge/Deep)\n- **T1-low** — Source reading without structural verification (Quick)\n- **T2** — QMD-enriched temporal context (Deep)\n- **T3** — External documentation, quarantined as untrusted\n\n### IDE Support\n\nClaude Code, Cursor, Cline, Codex, GitHub Copilot, Roo Code, Windsurf\n\n### Links\n\n- Documentation: <https://armelhbobdad.github.io/bmad-module-skill-forge>\n- npm: <https://www.npmjs.com/package/bmad-module-skill-forge>\n"
}
```
